Not invoked at construction time; evaluated by `CapabilityGates.check_all()` (T01.11). |\n13\t| `failure_mode` | `Literal[\"hard\", \"skip\", \"xfail\"]` | required | Gate tier per design-spec §11: `hard` aborts the run, `skip` marks gated evals SKIPPED, `xfail` marks them expected-fail. |\n14\t| `skip_flag` | `Optional[str]` | `None` | CLI flag (e.g. `--no-mcp`) that forces SOFT-SKIP behaviour even when the check would otherwise pass. |\n15\t| `description` | `str` | `\"\"` | Human-readable label used by `eval doctor` output. |\n16\t\n17\t## Invariants\n18\t\n19\t- `@dataclass(frozen=True)` — mutation raises `dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError`.\n20\t- `__post_init__` validates `failure_mode` membership against the literal\n21\t  set `{\"hard\", \"skip\", \"xfail\"}` and raises `ValueError` for any other\n22\t  value (including `\"HARD\"`, `\"hard \"`, `\"\"`, `\"soft\"`, etc.). This guards\n23\t  against typos in the static `CAPABILITIES` table that COMP-009 will\n24\t  declare in T01.11.\n25\t- Two instances built from identical arguments compare equal via the\n26\t  `@dataclass`-generated `__eq__`.\n27\t- The `check` callable is **not** invoked at construction time. Gate\n28\t  evaluation is deferred to `CapabilityGates.check_all()` so doctor\n29\t  output can stamp PASS/FAIL per capability rather than crashing at\n30\t  module import.\n31\t\n32\t## `failure_mode` semantics (design-spec §11 cross-reference)\n33\t\n34\t| Mode | Trigger | Run behaviour |\n35\t|---|---|---|\n36\t| `hard` | `check()` returns `False` for a HARD capability (e.g. `which D-0008 — Capability dataclass spec\n\n**Task:** T01.09 (Phase 1, Roadmap DM-007 / R-008)\n**Module:** `src/superclaude/cli/eval/capabilities.py`\n**Status:** Implemented 2026-05-20\n\n## Field schema (5-field contract)\n\n| Field | Type | Default | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `name` | `str` | required | Capability identifier (e.g. `binary.claude`, `mcp_server.auggie`). |\n| `check` | `Callable[[], bool]` | required | Zero-arg callable returning `True` when the capability is satisfied. Not invoked at construction time; evaluated by `CapabilityGates.check_all()` (T01.11). |\n| `failure_mode` | `Literal[\"hard\", \"skip\", \"xfail\"]` | required | Gate tier per design-spec §11: `hard` aborts the run, `skip` marks gated evals SKIPPED, `xfail` marks them expected-fail. |\n| `skip_flag` | `Optional[str]` | `None` | CLI flag (e.g. `--no-mcp`) that forces SOFT-SKIP behaviour even when the check would otherwise pass. |\n| `description` | `str` | `\"\"` | Human-readable label used by `eval doctor` output. |\n\n## Invariants\n\n- `@dataclass(frozen=True)` — mutation raises `dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError`.\n- `__post_init__` validates `failure_mode` membership against the literal\n  set `{\"hard\", \"skip\", \"xfail\"}` and raises `ValueError` for any other\n  value (including `\"HARD\"`, `\"hard \"`, `\"\"`, `\"soft\"`, etc.). This guards\n  against typos in the static `CAPABILITIES` table that COMP-009 will\n  declare in T01.11.\n- Two instances built from identical arguments compare equal via the\n  `@dataclass`-generated `__eq__`.\n- The `check` callable is **not** invoked at construction time. Gate\n  evaluation is deferred to `CapabilityGates.check_all()` so doctor\n  output can stamp PASS/FAIL per capability rather than crashing at\n  module import.\n\n## `failure_mode` semantics (design-spec §11 cross-reference)\n\n| Mode | Trigger | Run behaviour |\n|---|---|---|\n| `hard` | `check()` returns `False` for a HARD capability (e.g. `which claude` empty) | Abort run with exit code 2. |\n| `skip` | `check()` returns `False`, **or** the user passes the descriptor's `skip_flag` (e.g. `--no-mcp`) | Mark gated evals SKIPPED with reason; continue. |\n| `xfail` | Manifest declares `xfail_if:` and condition matched | Run, but expected-fail (status becomes XPASS if it passes anyway). |\n\n## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n\n- COMP-009 `CapabilityGates` (T01.11) — instantiates a static\n  `CAPABILITIES` list of these descriptors and iterates them in\n  `check_all()`.\n- `CapabilityReport` (T01.10) — collects PASS/FAIL per `Capability.name`\n  and groups blocked evals by `failure_mode`.\n- `eval doctor` (T01.13) — renders the green checklist using\n  `description` and `failure_mode`.\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T01.09) | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| Class `Capability` is a frozen dataclass exposing the 5 fields. | `capabilities.py` — `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Capability` |\n| Instantiation with an invalid `failure_mode` raises `ValueError`. | `Capability.__post_init__` (covered by `test_capability_rejects_invalid_failure_modes`). |\n| Two instances built from the same arguments compare equal. | `@dataclass`-generated `__eq__` (covered by `test_capability_deterministic_equality`). |\n| `spec.md` records the 5-field contract. | This file. |\n\n## Out of scope for T01.09\n\n- The static `CAPABILITIES` table (`binary.claude`, `binary.jq`,\n  `mcp_server.auggie`, …) — lives in COMP-009 (T01.11).\n- `CapabilityReport` aggregate model — T01.10 (DM-008).\n- `which_or_skip` / `mcp_server_reachable` implementations — T01.11.\n- Wiring of `--no-mcp` CLI flag — T01.13.\n","numLines":66,"startLine":1,"totalLines":66}}}

Concurrency model\n717\t\n718\t### Default: `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=8)`\n719\t\n720\tThe orchestrator uses `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor` (matching `cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`'s pattern, NOT the heavier `execution/parallel.py`). Each eval runs in its own thread; the thread blocks on `PtyDriver.wait()` which is itself a `pexpect` blocking read.\n721\t\n722\t### Isolation guarantees at high concurrency\n723\t\n724\t1. **No shared mutable state** — each eval has its own `HomeIsolation` with its own HOME dir.\n725\t2. **No shared file handles** — each eval opens its own `auggie-first.jsonl` under its HOME.\n726\t3. **No port collisions** — Claude Code TTY doesn't bind ports for the evals we run.\n727\t4. **Bounded process count** — at `--parallel 8`, max 8 `claude` subprocesses concurrently. At `--parallel 15`, 15. Each consumes ~150MB resident; 15-way needs ~2.25GB free.\n728\t\n729\t### Bounded retry (no infinite retry)\n730\t\n731\tFailed evals are NOT retried by default. The harness produces a deterministic single-pass run. The user can re-run with `--eval <failed-ids>` after diagnosing.\n732\t\n733\t### Signal handling\n734\t\n735\t- `SIGINT` (Ctrl-C): Cancel all running evals, mark in-flight as INTERRUPTED, write partial summary, exit 3.\n736\t- `SIGTERM`: Same as SIGINT.\n737\t- Per-eval timeout: kill the `PtyDriver` subprocess + reap zombie; mark eval as TIMEOUT (counts as FAIL).\n738\t\n739\t### What we DON'T do at high concurrency\n740\t\n741\t- No fancy async/await. Each eval is a blocking thread; the orchestrator just collects results via `as_completed`.\n742\t- No process supervisor / reaper daemon. Python's `concurrent.futures` cleans up worker threads on shutdown.\n743\t- No distributed mode. Single-host only.\n744\t\n745\t---\n746\t\n747\t## 13. Mitigation: **fork-and-own**.\n752\t\n753\t### Vendoring layout\n754\t\n755\t```\n756\tsrc/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/\n757\t├── __init__.py\n758\t├── driver.py        # PtyDriver (was: ptytest/__init__.py PtySession)\n759\t├── stream.py        # ANSI strip + line buffer (was: ptytest/_stream.py)\n760\t├── LICENSE          # upstream MIT, verbatim\n761\t└── PROVENANCE.md    # fork SHA, what we changed, why\n762\t```\n763\t\n764\t### What we change vs upstream\n765\t\n766\t| Change | Reason |\n767\t|---|---|\n768\t| Rename `PtySession` → `PtyDriver` | Avoid pytest-fixture connotation; we use it in a non-pytest context too. |\n769\t| Remove pytest-plugin entry-point | We don't need fixture autoloading. |\n770\t| Add `expect_prompt_ready(timeout=)` method | Cleaner waiting for Claude Code's TTY-prompt signal (the `>` or `$` indicator that input is welcome). |\n771\t| Add `inject_prompt(text)` method | Adds CR-LF + flushes — wraps stdin writes for Claude Code idioms. |\n772\t| Tighten dependency to `pexpect>=4.9` | The vendored ptytest doesn't pin pexpect tightly. |\n773\t| Apply ANSI-strip aggressively | Claude Code emits rich ANSI; the eval's TTY assertions need plain text. |\n774\t\n775\t### What we DON'T change\n776\t\n777\t- Core `pexpect.spawn` mechanics — leave it alone.\n778\t- The Docker-isolation mode (we don't need it; per-eval HOME isolation is enough).\n779\t- The original test suite — we vendor it under `tests/cli/test_eval/test_pty_vendor.py` to catch regressions during future ptytest re-syncs.\n780\t\n781\t### Upstream resync policy\n782\t\n783\tThe vendored fork is \"frozen at the fork SHA.\" If upstream releases significant changes (e.g., `pexpect` major-version bump, important bugfix), the `PROVENANCE.md` documents the resync procedure: pull upstream, three-way merge our changes, re-run vendored tests.\n784\t\n785\t### License compliance\n786\t\n787\tptytest is MIT. Concurrency model\n\n### Default: `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=8)`\n\nThe orchestrator uses `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor` (matching `cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`'s pattern, NOT the heavier `execution/parallel.py`). Each eval runs in its own thread; the thread blocks on `PtyDriver.wait()` which is itself a `pexpect` blocking read.\n\n### Isolation guarantees at high concurrency\n\n1. **No shared mutable state** — each eval has its own `HomeIsolation` with its own HOME dir.\n2. **No shared file handles** — each eval opens its own `auggie-first.jsonl` under its HOME.\n3. **No port collisions** — Claude Code TTY doesn't bind ports for the evals we run.\n4. **Bounded process count** — at `--parallel 8`, max 8 `claude` subprocesses concurrently. At `--parallel 15`, 15. Each consumes ~150MB resident; 15-way needs ~2.25GB free.\n\n### Bounded retry (no infinite retry)\n\nFailed evals are NOT retried by default. The harness produces a deterministic single-pass run. The user can re-run with `--eval <failed-ids>` after diagnosing.\n\n### Signal handling\n\n- `SIGINT` (Ctrl-C): Cancel all running evals, mark in-flight as INTERRUPTED, write partial summary, exit 3.\n- `SIGTERM`: Same as SIGINT.\n- Per-eval timeout: kill the `PtyDriver` subprocess + reap zombie; mark eval as TIMEOUT (counts as FAIL).\n\n### What we DON'T do at high concurrency\n\n- No fancy async/await. Each eval is a blocking thread; the orchestrator just collects results via `as_completed`.\n- No process supervisor / reaper daemon. Python's `concurrent.futures` cleans up worker threads on shutdown.\n- No distributed mode. Single-host only.\n\n---\n\n## 13. Fork strategy — vendored `ptytest`\n\n### What we fork\n\n`brandon-fryslie/ptytest` (MIT, Python 3.8+). The repo has 1 star / 0 forks — bus-factor risk is real. Mitigation: **fork-and-own**.\n\n### Vendoring layout\n\n```\nsrc/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/\n├── __init__.py\n├── driver.py        # PtyDriver (was: ptytest/__init__.py PtySession)\n├── stream.py        # ANSI strip + line buffer (was: ptytest/_stream.py)\n├── LICENSE          # upstream MIT, verbatim\n└── PROVENANCE.md    # fork SHA, what we changed, why\n```\n\n### What we change vs upstream\n\n| Change | Reason |\n|---|---|\n| Rename `PtySession` → `PtyDriver` | Avoid pytest-fixture connotation; we use it in a non-pytest context too. |\n| Remove pytest-plugin entry-point | We don't need fixture autoloading. |\n| Add `expect_prompt_ready(timeout=)` method | Cleaner waiting for Claude Code's TTY-prompt signal (the `>` or `$` indicator that input is welcome). |\n| Add `inject_prompt(text)` method | Adds CR-LF + flushes — wraps stdin writes for Claude Code idioms. |\n| Tighten dependency to `pexpect>=4.9` | The vendored ptytest doesn't pin pexpect tightly. |\n| Apply ANSI-strip aggressively | Claude Code emits rich ANSI; the eval's TTY assertions need plain text. |\n\n### What we DON'T change\n\n- Core `pexpect.spawn` mechanics — leave it alone.\n- The Docker-isolation mode (we don't need it; per-eval HOME isolation is enough).\n- The original test suite — we vendor it under `tests/cli/test_eval/test_pty_vendor.py` to catch regressions during future ptytest re-syncs.\n\n### Upstream resync policy\n\nThe vendored fork is \"frozen at the fork SHA.\" If upstream releases significant changes (e.g., `pexpect` major-version bump, important bugfix), the `PROVENANCE.md` documents the resync procedure: pull upstream, three-way merge our changes, re-run vendored tests.\n\n### License compliance\n\nptytest is MIT. We retain the upstream LICENSE verbatim in `cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` and reference it in IronClaude's top-level NOTICE (if one exists; otherwise add one).\n\n---\n\n## 14. This module stays declarative.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom typing import Any, Callable, Literal, Optional\n\nFailureMode = Literal[\"hard\", \"skip\", \"xfail\"]\n_VALID_FAILURE_MODES: frozenset[str] = frozenset({\"hard\", \"skip\", \"xfail\"})\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Capability:\n    \"\"\"Descriptor for a single capability check.\n\n    Equality is structural across all 5 fields (``@dataclass``-generated\n    ``__eq__``). Instances are hashable only when ``check`` is hashable —\n    ``shutil.which`` lambdas typically are, but callers should not rely on\n    Capability being a dict key.\n\n    Construction with an invalid ``failure_mode`` raises ``ValueError``\n    via ``__post_init__`` so a typo in a manifest or in the static\n    ``CAPABILITIES`` list fails loudly at import time rather than at gate\n    evaluation time.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    check: Callable[[], bool]\n    failure_mode: FailureMode\n    skip_flag: Optional[str] = None\n    description: str = \"\"\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        if self.failure_mode not in _VALID_FAILURE_MODES:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"Capability.failure_mode must be one of \"\n                f\"{sorted(_VALID_FAILURE_MODES)!r}; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_012fibnrEfCEe7hbLbNa8m93","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01F9LVZu3kzVNFPjQgU36moY","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0009/notes.md","content":"# D-0009 — Implementation notes\n\n## Design choices\n\n1. **Tuples instead of lists.** A frozen dataclass with mutable list\n   defaults would be either (a) unhashable, or (b) a foot-gun if a\n   caller passed in a shared list. `tuple` plus\n   `field(default_factory=tuple)` gives an immutable, hashable report\n   while remaining trivially JSON-serialisable (json.dumps converts\n   tuples to arrays anyway, and `to_json()` materialises lists\n   explicitly for clarity).\n\n2. **Why a dedicated `CapabilityStatus` instead of `dict`s?**\n   `report[]` carries structured per-capability outcomes that the\n   doctor renders into a green checklist (design-spec §11). Using a\n   dataclass:\n   - matches the pattern set by `Capability` (T01.09) and `EvalSpec`\n     (T01.03),\n   - documents the row shape at the type level rather than in\n     freeform dicts,\n   - lets `to_json()` produce stable key ordering via an explicit\n     `to_dict()` method instead of relying on Python dict insertion\n     order from arbitrary call sites.\n\n3. **`to_json()` returns `dict`, not `str`.** DM-008 says \"serializable\n   to JSON\" and the validation report (L2 patch) demoted byte-level\n   determinism to a Notes-level concern. Returning a mapping lets\n   callers compose it (e.g. doctor wrapping it in a larger envelope)\n   without re-parsing.\n\n4. **`skipped_by_flag` field on `CapabilityStatus`.** Distinguishes\n   \"capability genuinely failed\" from \"capability passed but the user\n   asked us to ignore it (`--no-mcp`)\". COMP-009 (T01.11) sets this\n   bit when it sees a `Capability.skip_flag` in the active skip set.\n\n5. **Key ordering.** `to_json()` emits keys in dataclass-field order,\n   not alphabetical, so the JSON layout mirrors how a reader would read\n   the dataclass definition. This makes diffs in doctor snapshot tests\n   meaningful (see T01.13 Notes about derived determinism).\n\n## Trade-offs and follow-ups\n\n- **No reverse `from_dict()`.** DM-008 only requires serialisation,\n  not round-tripping. If a future test asserts JSON → `CapabilityReport`\n  parity we can add `from_json()` then; right now it would be\n  speculative.\n- **Tuple ordering is caller-controlled.** `CapabilityGates.check_all`\n  (T01.11) is responsible for emitting capabilities in the canonical\n  `CAPABILITIES` order, which is what gives the doctor output its\n  stable shape. `CapabilityReport` does not sort defensively.\n- **`Capability` not used as a `report[]` entry.** A `Capability`\n  holds a `Callable`, which is not JSON-safe. Hence the dedicated\n  `CapabilityStatus` value type.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2383,"cache_read_input_tokens":115951,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2383,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":54,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"48cdac4d-3cda-49c8-a0c3-7e09a438e1f3","uuid":"28874cd2-e4da-4f8b-af65-ed8b19d747c4"}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01F9LVZu3kzVNFPjQgU36moY","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0009/notes.md (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"48cdac4d-3cda-49c8-a0c3-7e09a438e1f3","uuid":"3386fb11-831c-43d2-bcbf-741fa5f43157","timestamp":"2026-05-20T05:34:09.453Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"create","filePath":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0009/notes.md","content":"# D-0009 — Implementation notes\n\n## Design choices\n\n1. **Tuples instead of lists.** A frozen dataclass with mutable list\n   defaults would be either (a) unhashable, or (b) a foot-gun if a\n   caller passed in a shared list. `tuple` plus\n   `field(default_factory=tuple)` gives an immutable, hashable report\n   while remaining trivially JSON-serialisable (json.dumps converts\n   tuples to arrays anyway, and `to_json()` materialises lists\n   explicitly for clarity).\n\n2. **Why a dedicated `CapabilityStatus` instead of `dict`s?**\n   `report[]` carries structured per-capability outcomes that the\n   doctor renders into a green checklist (design-spec §11). Using a\n   dataclass:\n   - matches the pattern set by `Capability` (T01.09) and `EvalSpec`\n     (T01.03),\n   - documents the row shape at the type level rather than in\n     freeform dicts,\n   - lets `to_json()` produce stable key ordering via an explicit\n     `to_dict()` method instead of relying on Python dict insertion\n     order from arbitrary call sites.\n\n3. **`to_json()` returns `dict`, not `str`.** DM-008 says \"serializable\n   to JSON\" and the validation report (L2 patch) demoted byte-level\n   determinism to a Notes-level concern. Returning a mapping lets\n   callers compose it (e.g. doctor wrapping it in a larger envelope)\n   without re-parsing.\n\n4. **`skipped_by_flag` field on `CapabilityStatus`.** Distinguishes\n   \"capability genuinely failed\" from \"capability passed but the user\n   asked us to ignore it (`--no-mcp`)\". COMP-009 (T01.11) sets this\n   bit when it sees a `Capability.skip_flag` in the active skip set.\n\n5. **Key ordering.** `to_json()` emits keys in dataclass-field order,\n   not alphabetical, so the JSON layout mirrors how a reader would read\n   the dataclass definition. This makes diffs in doctor snapshot tests\n   meaningful (see T01.13 Notes about derived determinism).\n\n## Trade-offs and follow-ups\n\n- **No reverse `from_dict()`.** DM-008 only requires serialisation,\n  not round-tripping. If a future test asserts JSON → `CapabilityReport`\n  parity we can add `from_json()` then; right now it would be\n  speculative.\n- **Tuple ordering is caller-controlled.** `CapabilityGates.check_all`\n  (T01.11) is responsible for emitting capabilities in the canonical\n  `CAPABILITIES` order, which is what gives the doctor output its\n  stable shape. `CapabilityReport` does not sort defensively.\n- **`Capability` not used as a `report[]` entry.** A `Capability`\n  holds a `Callable`, which is not JSON-safe. Hence the dedicated\n  `CapabilityStatus` value type.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}
